# Proposed OEIS comments: A210103 to A219454

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A210103

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A210103>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [3036](../papers/03001-03500/03036-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 39*a(n-1) -424*a(n-2) -100*a(n-3) +25342*a(n-4) -98910*a(n-5) -353224*a(n-6) +2683456*a(n-7) -679949*a(n-8) -24899773*a(n-9) +39419608*a(n-10) +84219756*a(n-11) -230024712*a(n-12) -56603460*a(n-13) +492890768*a(n-14) -143861456*a(n-15) -452015392*a(n-16) +208017984*a(n-17) +175455296*a(n-18) -65216128*a(n-19) -32317440*a(n-20) +2433024*a(n-21) +925696*a(n-22)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 58 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 21. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11105](../papers/11001-11500/11105-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 12*a(n-1) - 7*a(n-2) - 108*a(n-3) + 22*a(n-4) + 258*a(n-5) + 124*a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(39 - 62*x - 399*x^2 + 173*x^3 + 1065*x^4 + 496*x^5) / ((1 + 2*x)*(1 - 14*x + 35*x^2 + 38*x^3 - 98*x^4 - 62*x^5)). - _Colin Barker_, Jul 15 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 16 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A210474

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A210474>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [13164](../papers/13001-13500/13164-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: (n+1)*a(n) +2*(-5*n+3)*a(n-1) +9*(n-3)*a(n-2)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 2, so the recurrence holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A210781

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A210781>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [2573](../papers/02501-03000/02573-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-2) +2*a(n-3) +4*a(n-4) +38*a(n-5) +106*a(n-6) +172*a(n-7) +596*a(n-8) +573*a(n-9) +971*a(n-10) +744*a(n-11) -1425*a(n-12) -4335*a(n-13) -10525*a(n-14) -11517*a(n-15) -13462*a(n-16) -6001*a(n-17) -4772*a(n-18) +619*a(n-19) +10548*a(n-20) +45667*a(n-21) +64053*a(n-22) +34800*a(n-23) -11095*a(n-24) -26829*a(n-25) +41629*a(n-26) +81493*a(n-27) -35792*a(n-28) -237880*a(n-29) -186310*a(n-30) +61561*a(n-31) +67920*a(n-32) -147561*a(n-33) -221267*a(n-34) -90437*a(n-35) -13145*a(n-36) +33145*a(n-37) +101632*a(n-38) +63235*a(n-39) +3056*a(n-40) -2932*a(n-41) -6086*a(n-42) -5505*a(n-43) +115*a(n-44) +649*a(n-45) +319*a(n-46) +258*a(n-47) +4*a(n-48) -24*a(n-49) for n>51.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition ranges over the whole array but reaches only as far as the value itself, which the alphabet bounds, so carrying that many lines together with one bit per cell -- has this cell found its partner yet -- makes the count a walk count on 99 states. Hence a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and checking the one above is a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 51. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A212205

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A212205>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [12916](../papers/12501-13000/12916-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence: (n+1)*a(n) +2*(n-1)*a(n-1) +6*(-2*n+1)*a(n-2) +12*(-2*n+5)*a(n-3) +(37*n-77)*a(n-4) +2*(37*n-151)*a(n-5) +6*(-n+5)*a(n-6) +12*(-n+7)*a(n-7)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Aug 20 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 6, so the recurrence holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A212925

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A212925>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [5362](../papers/05001-05500/05362-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 37*a(n-1) -279*a(n-2) +243*a(n-3).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(5 + 99*x) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 9*x)*(1 - 27*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Jul 21 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. "In all rows" quantifies over the whole array, so no bounded window decides it, but it is a conjunction of independent per-row facts: keep one bit per adjacent column pair, recording whether every row so far has put column j above column j-1. A row can only clear bits, and the array is admissible exactly when all are clear at the end. So the state is that bit mask alone, giving 3 states, however large the alphabet, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A213203

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A213203>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [12356](../papers/12001-12500/12356-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: a(n) + (-n^2-n-11)*a(n-1) + (n^3+7*n^2-13*n+39)*a(n-2) - 2*(n-2)*(4*n^2-2*n-15)*a(n-3) + 20*(n-2)*(n-3)*(n-4)*a(n-4) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 21 2013

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical: a(n) = 2^n/9 +3/16 -53*(-1)^n/144 +3^n/16 +5^n/144. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jul 09 2012
> Empirical: G.f.: x*(1 - 9*x + 24*x^2 - 19*x^3) / ((1 - x)*(1 + x)*(1 - 2*x)*(1 - 3*x)*(1 - 5*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Apr 22 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 21 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A217358

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A217358>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [13307](../papers/13001-13500/13307-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 46*n*(n-1)*(n-2)*a(n) -(n-1)*(n-2)*(11*n-74)*a(n-1) -(n-2)*(336*n^2-1359*n+1351)*a(n-2) +(-347*n^3+2190*n^2-3861*n+1330)*a(n-3) + 8*(2*n-7)*(4*n-15)*(4*n-17)*a(n-4) = 0.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A218185

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A218185>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [12393](../papers/12001-12500/12393-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*(n-1)*(n-2)*a(n) -12*(2*n-5)*(n-1)*(n-2)*a(n-1) +4 *(n-2)*(52*n^2-312*n+465) *a(n-2) -48*(2*n-7)*(8*n^2-56*n+95)*a(n-3) +256*(n-5) *(2*n-7) *(2*n-9)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Oct 08 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 4, so the recurrence holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A218348

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A218348>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4711](../papers/04501-05000/04711-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 21 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A219314

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A219314>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [12980](../papers/12501-13000/12980-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*a(n) -2*n*a(n-1) +11*(-n+2)*a(n-2) +4*(2*n-5)*a(n-3) +8*(5*n-17)*a(n-4) +24*(n-4)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016
> Conjecture: n*(5*n-7)*a(n) -4*(5*n^2-12*n+6)*a(n-1) -(15*n^2-11*n-30) *a(n-2) +2*(35*n^2-119*n+66)*a(n-3) +12*(n-3)*(5*n2)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 3, so the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A219454

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (1/40320)*n^8 - (1/2016)*n^7 + (7/2880)*n^6 + (53/720)*n^5 - (6313/5760)*n^4 + (1961/288)*n^3 - (170417/10080)*n^2 + (3393/280)*n + 13 for n>5.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Mar 11 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical closed form is correct. The count is a walk count in a finite graph, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; the conjectured form satisfies the same one, and the two agree at enough consecutive indices to agree from then on, for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
